| 1 | `/dev-team:idea-refine I want to start a personal quant research platform for market research on US equities, built in stages. Let's refine the idea and plan it until it's ready to build.

Here is everything I eventually want it to do:
1. Stage 1 — Data: a reliable daily US equity dataset and loader (prices, returns, corporate actions, point-in-time universe), from my Sharadar subscription and cross-checked against Yahoo Finance through yahooquery, with scripts to download, clean, validate, and store it, plus basic exploratory analysis, and a data-viz app to explore it: a TypeScript + React browser frontend over a Python backend (HTTP and WebSocket).
2. Stage 2 — Return structure & classical portfolios, no ML: PCA decomposition of returns to remove the market (and other dominant) factors, Marchenko–Pastur random-matrix denoising of the correlation matrix, and covariance estimation (sample, shrinkage, denoised) compared on real data. Then minimum-variance, risk parity and hierarchical risk parity built on those estimates, compared out of sample on realized variance. Get variance optimization right before any return prediction.
3. Stage 3 — Features & ML: build candidate features, then test them with ML for information gain over the residual (market-removed) returns. Every feature claim must pass a proper null-hypothesis test so we don't fool ourselves; this needs scripting and real data analysis.
4. Stage 4 — Forecasting & trading agents: return/volatility forecasting with neural networks (e.g. sequence models), built on the features that survived stage 3 and compared against simple forecasting baselines. Then portfolios driven by those forecasts, and PPO trading/allocation agents trained in a backtest environment. Both must beat the stage-2 classical portfolios out of sample, paper trading only.

I've put my reference code for this in references/: market_decomposition.py, tft_dataset.py, tft_targets.py.` | D1–D27 | U01, U02, U03 |
| 2 | `/dev-team:idea-refine From the previous session:
<<<
ief. That's deliberate: they're recorded word for word and go into the spec next.

**Stages to add on acceptance.** Each lists its skills and references:

| Stage | Outline | Skills | References |
|---|---|---|---|
| **S1: Data** | Download, clean, validate and store the as-of daily universe, cross-check it against Yahoo Finance, run the exploratory analysis, and browse it in the app, with live progress for long runs | data-analysis, dataviz, frontend-design | — |
| **S2: Return structure & classical portfolios** | Remove the market factors, denoise and compare covariance estimators, and rank minimum-variance, risk parity and HRP on out-of-sample variance after costs. This builds the shared out-of-sample testing harness | data-analysis, dataviz | RF-01 |
| **S3: Features & ML** | Candidate features tested for information gain over market-removed returns, each with a null-hypothesis test, a correction for how many features were tried, and a recorded verdict | null-hypothesis-testing, data-analysis | — |
| **S4: Forecasting & trading agents** | Neural return and volatility forecasts against simple baselines, then forecast-driven portfolios and PPO agents in a backtest, held to the bar "beat the S2 portfolios after costs, or document the loss," then simulated paper trading | null-hypothesis-testing, data-analysis | RF-02, RF-03 |

**Accept the brief and add these stages with these skills and references?** [yes / edit] On a yes I'll also offer to commit `docs/` and `references/`.
>>>

| 4 | `/dev-team:architecture-design From the previous session:
<<<
The spec is approved, dated 2026-10-18, and all four roadmap stages now show as Specced.

Your answers and what changed:
- **QR-05:** rented GPU time is capped at $10 per training run. Each run states its expected hours and cost before it starts, and the expected cost must be $10 or less.
- **FPORT-02 and AGENT-04:** forecast-driven portfolios and the trading agents are now judged on Sharpe ratio after costs against the stage-2 benchmark.
- **AGENT-01:** the agents' reward now includes a risk penalty, so an episode's total reward tracks its Sharpe ratio after costs. Your yes to question 3 covered the risk penalty as well as the comparison metric, so I wrote that into AGENT-01. Tell me if you meant only the comparison metric.
- **QR-02 and QR-03:** confirmed as written; no text change.
- **Assumptions:** the eight are now listed as confirmed.
- **Open questions:** only the three data-source questions remain, and probes will answer them during architecture.

I recorded your answers as requests UR-39 to UR-42 and linked each to the requirements above. No spec request is left open, and lint shows no errors.

Nothing is committed yet. Your edits are in `docs/spec.md`, `docs/requests.md` and `docs/roadmap.md`. Do you want me to commit them? The architecture comes next, starting next turn.
>>>

Yes, commit. The risk penalty in AGENT-01 is what I meant, so keep it as written.` | D61–D116 | — |

## Driver (whole main thread)

- `D1` **USER TYPED** `/dev-team:idea-refine I want to start a personal quant research platform for market research on US equities, built in stages. Let's refine the idea and plan it until it's ready to build.

Here is everything I eventually want it to do:
1. Stage 1 — Data: a reliable daily US equity dataset and loader (prices, returns, corporate actions, point-in-time universe), from my Sharadar subscription and cross-checked against Yahoo Finance through yahooquery, with scripts to download, clean, validate, and store it, plus basic exploratory analysis, and a data-viz app to explore it: a TypeScript + React browser frontend over a Python backend (HTTP and WebSocket).
2. Stage 2 — Return structure & classical portfolios, no ML: PCA decomposition of returns to remove the market (and other dominant) factors, Marchenko–Pastur random-matrix denoising of the correlation matrix, and covariance estimation (sample, shrinkage, denoised) compared on real data. Then minimum-variance, risk parity and hierarchical risk parity built on those estimates, compared out of sample on realized variance. Get variance optimization right before any return prediction.
3. Stage 3 — Features & ML: build candidate features, then test them with ML for information gain over the residual (market-removed) returns. Every feature claim must pass a proper null-hypothesis test so we don't fool ourselves; this needs scripting and real data analysis.
4. Stage 4 — Forecasting & trading agents: return/volatility forecasting with neural networks (e.g. sequence models), built on the features that survived stage 3 and compared against simple forecasting baselines. Then portfolios driven by those forecasts, and PPO trading/allocation agents trained in a backtest environment. Both must beat the stage-2 classical portfolios out of sample, paper trading only.

I've put my reference code for this in references/: market_decomposition.py, tft_dataset.py, tft_targets.py.`
